Method, once: nntrainer_causallm on the phone, NNTR_NUM_THREADS=8, prompt 512 tokens, generation 64 / 512 / 1024, two runs per cell, the non-profile binary. Decode tok/s is the generation: line (whole generation). Every sitting starts with variant A, the unchanged reference, and results are read as an A/B inside that sitting. Any Galaxy S25 Ultra may run a handoff (user decision 2026-09-22): a handoff names no serial, the filled handoff records the serial that was used, every row below carries it, and numbers are compared only inside one sitting; where two sittings happen to share a unit the drift is recorded as a note. BENCHMARK.md "Method"; contract §1.1 and §12 (2026-09-22); LEDGER rule 13

Added since cycle 21. (1) Temperature is part of the row: the G=1024 cell moves ±6 % with the start temperature on one unit within one day (rule 52), so the row of record is quoted with its condition — cool start, zone0 ≤ 35 °C per G block — and a warm-start reading of the same default is reported next to it. (2) Bit preservation is the gate for the hybrid levers (MoE dumps bit_identical=1 and text ≡ A on the 8-prompt set); a new repetition loop fails a variant even when its decode PPL passes (rule 45). (3) Who runs the phone: agents ran the sittings with adb between 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01 (#201, #207, #216); since 2026-10-02 no device is attached and the user runs every handoff from a device-farm session; the S25 column is frozen (unit withdrawn 2026-10-01), the S26 column opens with a product unit. (4) Pool-miss cells (the E2E path with an expert pool) carry the decode window's pgpgin or PSI io next to the ms/miss (rule 61 amended). What it means, in plain words. At decode a projection's time is the time to read its weights. P4 gave the one-PD token's projections (FC) and dense FFN a copy of their weights in the DSP's own layout, so the DSP stops reading the CPU's Q4_0 bytes through its slower CPU-exact kernel. That bought 5.6 ms of a 62 ms token, +8–9 % at every length. The bytes shrank only 10.6 % (both copies are 4 bits a weight); most of the gain is the read rate, 40.8 → 49.4 MiB/ms, which is now close to what the DMA can do. So the next milliseconds must come from fewer bytes: 2-bit FCs (#229 S2), −8.0 ms by plan 229 §8.1's arithmetic. The hybrid, which leaves those weights to the CPU, is still faster (18.37 against 15.89 at gen 512). The flag: the dummy has no full-precision FCs, so its sidecar was made by re-quantizing Q4_0 weights, two roundings on two different rulers (page 2 §2.9). The tokens pass the near-tie policy, but only because the logits moved by much more than E16's did: at every differing step F prefers its token by 1.08–1.37 logits where A had the two tied. The implementer did not apply the +2 % perplexity rule on a dummy and said so. The supervisor agreed for these cells and recorded that a real checkpoint with Q4_0 FCs would hit the same stacking (㊸). The user's answer is option A: the producer writes per-column FCs. A second, kernel-side cause (the u8 rounding of each input row, rule 73) stays on any model and is #258's question. 234-p4-fcwh-decode.md §Step 2–3; BENCHMARK Method (cycle-42 paragraph), "#234 P4 side tables"; LEDGER cycle 42 (1)–(5), rule 73, ㊸; plan 229 §8.1–§8.2; issue #258

What it means, in plain words. This is the first time the 26B model ran on the phone, and the sitting was about the path, not the words: the weights are a dummy, so the generated text means nothing and no accuracy can be read from it. What it shows: the whole decode step runs as one DSP call per token, with two attention caches (25 sliding-window layers at head width 256, 5 full layers at 512); where the two runs can be compared exactly they agree — the 30 prefill MoE calls are bit-identical to the reference — and where they cannot (in E the projections, attention, norms and head run on the DSP, in the reference on the CPU; rule 39) the tokens differ only at steps whose two best candidates the reference itself barely separates (top-1 minus top-2 0.0003–0.022, against a run-to-run logit noise of 0.26; plan 130 §3.5's near-tie rule). The user read that as pass (a). The speed is plain: the one-call path is ≈ 30 % slower than the hybrid at every length, and the expert pool is not why — 8, 16, 32 or 40 slots give the same rate. The DSP spends ≈ 30 of its 63 ms a token in the three kinds that read Q4_0 weights through its CPU-exact path (FC, LM_HEAD, DENSE_FFN): 1278.7 MiB in ≈ 30 ms is ≈ 43 GB/s, while in the hybrid the phone's CPU reads those same bytes at 51–63 GB/s (rule 46). So the next deliverable is fewer FC bytes on the DSP: #234 P3 / P4, the FC WH sidecar (≈ half the bytes of Q4M1 per FC), then #229 S2, 2-bit FCs. Two load-time findings came with it: the CPU's RoPE table was sized for 262 144 positions (1.5 GiB) and killed the first run (rule 72, #253), and the largest pool is set by the DSP heap, not by the 3840 MiB map (rule 71). 201-s5-0-gemma-dummy.md §Step 1–3; BENCHMARK Method (cycle-40 paragraph), Goals (Gemma row), S5-0 side tables; LEDGER cycle 40, §2 #201 S5-0 row, rules 71–72, §3 ㉘ (cycle-40 note), ㊷; contract §12 (2026-10-07, cycle 39–40)

What it means, in plain words. The goal was decode ≥ 50 tok/s on the NPU at every generation length, and faster than the same phone's CPU. On 2026-09-29 one bit on the weight DMA (do not route the source reads through the DSP's L2 cache) let the DMA engine read the expert weights at ≈ 57 GB/s instead of ≈ 33, and decode jumped from ≈ 37 to ≈ 51 tok/s with every output byte unchanged (#158). After that the only cell under 50 was G=1024, and the day's many control runs showed why: it read 50.9–52.4 when the phone started cool and 45.9–49.1 when it started warm. The record sitting settled it the way the method demands — A alone, twice per length, each length started below 35 °C — and read 53.97 / 52.16 / 51.41. The CPU control, run the same way on the same phone minutes later, read 52.18 / 51.31 / 50.12, so the NPU path is ahead at every length, by 1.7–3.4 %. The project's work since then (cycles 24–30) is the end-to-end path — every decode op on the DSP, one call per token — which is bit-identical but still slower than this hybrid default (42.9 vs 51.3 at G=512 in the same sitting), and the last S25 sittings found that its slow expert misses are the model file being read back from flash after Android evicted it from memory (#216). record-2026-09-30-cool-a.md (Method, md5, Results, Thermal, CPU control, Verdict); BENCHMARK.md Method (cycle-22, cycle-23, record-sitting and cycle-27 paragraphs), Goals (NPU, CPU and prefill rows), Results (record sitting, #158, #201, #207, #216 rows), Log cycle 30; 216-fadvise.md @ 45be8de65 (A/B, Gate); LEDGER rules 43, 52, 59, 61, 62; contract §12 (2026-10-01, 2026-10-02)

What it means, in plain words. Every decoded token makes 22 calls from the ARM into the DSP, one per MoE layer. Until now each was a FastRPC call: the kernel driver marshals the arguments, wakes a DSP thread, runs the function and wakes the ARM again — 87.9 µs of pure transport per call on this phone, ≈ 1.9 ms per token. A microbench in the #134 sitting (PR #147) had measured the alternative, the SDK's dspqueue: a shared-memory message queue that a DSP thread watches. With both sides spinning (checking the queue in a tight loop) a round trip carrying the same 12 KiB took 16.7 µs against FastRPC's 87.1. #141 step 2 moved the model's 22 M==1 MoE calls onto such a queue while running the same DSP function on the same argument bytes, so nothing the DSP computes could change — and on the phone nothing did: every one of 2862 MoE dump files is bit-identical to the FastRPC run, and all eight prompts of the new text set produce the same text. The transport fell to 14.1 µs per call and decode rose +7.1 % at gen 64 and +5.0 % at gen 512. Letting the DSP thread sleep between calls instead (Q0) kept only part of it — the 1 ms spin window is what buys the latency. The user made it the default (PR #151, NNTR_HTP_DSPQ=0 brings FastRPC back). Why this moves the "now" without a control sitting, unlike #117's feed: the change is bit-identical to A at every MoE call, so a control sitting could only re-read the transport this sitting already read as an A/B; the flip commit changes nothing but the unset value of the switch, and its banner was checked on the phone. What it leaves: the MoE call now has ≤ 0.9 ms left in it (the dsp − mm tail and the transport); the weights run at the in-app DMA floor (#149, rule 41). The other ≈ 10.8 ms of a 26.5 ms token is the CPU between the calls (next section, and #150).

The build trap found on the way
no ENABLE_HEXKL
Applications/CausalLM/jni/Android.mk never defines it, so every #ifdef ENABLE_HEXKL block of the app (the op list, set_decode_graph_desc, the four decode hooks) compiled out of every device app since #85; libnntrainer.so and the skel had the define. Rule 36: a missing banner is void, never "at A's speed"; grep the app library for NNTR_HTP_FORWARD_KINDS. Fixed by #135 / PR #139 (02d96eb3, cycle 20): the define is exported with the prebuilt and build_android.sh does the grep itself; #136's sitting is the first device use

What it means, in plain words. Since 2026-09-23 the tree has had a second door into the DSP: instead of the ARM calling the NPU once per MoE layer (22 calls per token), it can hand the DSP a description of the whole decode step and let the DSP run every op it has a kernel for before returning. PR #119 built the door with only the MoE kernel behind it; PRs #125 / #126 landed kernels for the norms, RoPE, the conv1d + gate and the decode attention; PR #133 (#130) put those five behind the door too and added a mask to open it for "MoE only". Nothing of that had run on a phone. This sitting asked three questions and answered all three. Does the door itself cost anything? No: C, the entry with MOE alone resident, reads +6.8 / +5.1 / +0.3 % against A — inside a warm phone's own spread — with byte-identical text. Plan 85's B, never measured until now, is free. Does putting the five small kinds behind the door pay? Not as wired, and by more than the plan expected: B reads −22 / −25 / −30 %. The op list still has an FC, an ADD or the router on the ARM between every resident stretch and the MoE op, so instead of fewer calls there are more — 95 per token — and the profile shows where the time goes: 20.9 ms of DSP time per token instead of 15.7, of which the attention kernel alone is 0.79 of a MoE op at pos 512, plus 73 extra round trips. The ride-along timed that kernel at ≈ 1.05 µs per KV position per layer — 3.2 ms per token at pos 512, 7.1 at pos 1024 — which is exactly why the loss widens with the generation length. Is the text right? No: with all six kinds on, the model leaves A's text at the first word and loops the same sentence; the user rejected it (#136). Rule 37 says why it is not the few-ulp differences the gtests found on subnormal rows: those cannot move a logit, so #136 is a logic or binding fault (the KV seed after the CPU prefill, the RoPE position offset at pos ≥ 512, the conv state seed, a graph_set_param binding the tiny host fixture never exercised). Two more things were learned on the way. The committed Android app had never compiled its NNTR_HTP_FORWARD code at all — the first pass of B and C ran at A's speed with no graph: line — so this sitting ran on a local rebuild with the define added by hand (#135, queued first), and the phone was warm from that first pass, which is why A sits 8–11 % under #120 A and why the "now" stays #120 A. For the goal: the ⑨ track is now measured on silicon and, as wired, costs +8.5 … +13.9 ms per token where it must deliver ≈ −5; the call count (㉓, #132) and the attention kernel's per-position cost (㉗) are the two terms that must fall, and neither is worth a sitting before B's text passes. Queue: #135 → #136 → #132; #134 (a decode-side PPL, since NNTR_PPL scores prefill only) p2.

What it means, in plain words. Two questions rode one sitting. First, the housekeeping BENCHMARK's rules demanded: the VTCM feed had won +29–32 % in #117 as a lever (variant B against an arena-read control), and PR #118 made it the default, but a lever cell never becomes the row of record — so the flipped default had to run as a sitting's own unchanged A, with nothing set, against A0 = the same binaries with the feed switched off. It did: A 35.97 / 35.96 / 35.17 tok/s, A0 28.62 at G=64, +25.7 %, mm 677 vs 934 µs, text identical; and A's numbers sit within −3.8 / −1.5 / +0.5 % of what the same code read as #117's B on the same phone, which is ordinary same-unit drift. So BENCHMARK's "now" moved from 28.22 / 27.89 / 27.23 to 35.97 / 35.96 / 35.17, and the distance to 50 tok/s from 1.79× to 1.39×. Second, the user's decision to take every commit of upstream PR #4327 (40 commits — NNTR_PPL, the conv block as one call, the dense FFN through the MoE kernel, the fused qkv layer, the down matmul one block behind the gate_up, the pooled FC epilogue) onto htp_moe as one PR, #121: the merge had re-derived the M=1 GEMV path — one-row loop, 192 KB lead, VTCM feed — on top of upstream's rewritten block loop and was host-verified only. Its gate was "decode ≥ −2 % of A at each G, prefill ≥ −5 %, text identical". Decode read +4.74 / +0.92 / −0.19 % — unmoved: the M==1 profile rows are the same to the microsecond (dsp 711.7 vs 711.3, mm 677.2 vs 676.6), and the +4.7 % at G=64 is A's own first cell after the profiles reading low. Prefill read +12.4 / +10.2 / +6.2 %, the first variant in this project to raise it, because two of upstream's changes are on by default at M > 1: the M>1 DSP time fell 12.8 %, almost all of it in requant and dequant. Text identical at every G, the GEMV bit-identical on B's skel. Gate passed, and PR #121 merged on 2026-09-27 as b7d46b57 — after a rebase over htp_moe @ b4c96bdb (PR #119, the per-token entry) with host gates 0–1 only and no new sitting, by user decision: the per-token entry is off by default, so B's measured path is unchanged by the rebase, and this sitting stands as the PR's device evidence. From here on the unchanged control binary of any sitting is this B; BENCHMARK's Method says to read the next A against #120 A (prefill ≈ +10 % expected, decode unmoved). Both skels here are older than PR #119: neither carries the graph entries, and no skel built after the merge has a recorded md5 yet (the merged PRs were opened with rungs 0–1 only; LEDGER §3a). generation(last 64) is not in these logs; PR #128 (merged the same day) prints it from the next handoff on.

201-s5-0-gemma-dummy.md §Step 2; BENCHMARK "#201 S5-0 side tables"; LEDGER §2 #201 S5-0 row, ㊷

Where the 19.7 ms of a token go now (cycles 22–27, measured with NNTR_OP_TIME=1 on the hybrid path; rt from the token driver on the E2E path)

From cycle 22 the split is a measurement, not an estimate: PR #153's per-node decode timer (NNTR_OP_TIME=1) rides the tok/s binary and is inert (#150: T8 within A0's spread, MoE dumps bit-identical). On the dspqueue default before the bypass a G=512 token was 26.22 ms = MoE wait 16.08 + ≈ 10.1 on the CPU (rule 46). The DSP L2 bypass took the MoE wait to 9.85 ms and the token to 19.74 ms (#158 B r1, G=512), i.e. under the 20 ms that 50 tok/s needs. The CPU half (≈ 9.9 ms) is now half the token and near its byte floor: the Q4_0 FCs stream at 51–63 GB/s, FC + lm_head 7.4 ms for 402 MB, attention 1.47 ms at G=512 and 1.97 at G=1024 (#90), norms, adds, conv1d and sampling ≈ 0.6 (rule 46). At G=1024 from a cool start (#162 step 0 A) the token reads 19.10–19.65 ms with attention 1.68–1.87 and MoE wait 9.5 — 50 tok/s with almost nothing to spare, which is why the start temperature decides G=1024 (rule 52). Two readers cannot buy more: the phone's DRAM tops out at ≈ 70 GB/s for any mix, and the DSP alone takes ≈ 57 of it during the MoE window (rules 44, 43). On the one-PD E2E path (every op on the DSP, one call per token, pool C = 28) the token driver's round trip reads 22.4 ms against 26.6 for two PDs with the same pool and 30.6 for two PDs all-resident (rule 59).

Earlier budget — where the 26.5 ms of a token went (cycle 21, from #141 Q — estimate, derived from tok/s — not measured)

Same arithmetic as before (LEDGER §2 and BENCHMARK "Goals", NPU status cell): the token time of the tok/s binary at G=512 (1000 / 37.77 = 26.48 ms) minus 22 × the level-2 M==1 host of Q's G=64 profile (714.1 µs = dsp 700.0 + transport 14.1). Two ledger rules changed how the floors read: rule 41 (#149: in the app the DMA engine reads ≈ 0.88 × its isolated anchor, so the MoE floor on this unit is 22 × ≈ 0.670 = 14.7 ms, not 13.0) and rule 42 (the per-token byte count: outside the MoE call the CPU reads ≈ 402 MB of Q4_0 FC + lm_head, not ≈ 246).

What would move it now. (1) Inside the MoE call: nothing worth a PR. The weights run at the in-app floor (#149), the transport is 0.3 ms (#141); ≤ 0.9 ms is left in tail + transport together. (2) The CPU between the calls (#150, p1, the queue head): it already reads its ≈ 402 MB at ≥ 37–41 GB/s; the thread count alone was worth ≤ 0.5 ms (#150's first read), so the plan measures a per-op byte rate first (PR #153, NNTR_OP_TIME) and then tries at most two order-preserving levers — up to ≈ 4–5 ms if the GEMVs reached the CPU's single-reader 67.9 GB/s. (3) Not a lever: moving the Q4_0 FCs onto the DSP — it reads slower in the app (≤ 33 GB/s ⇒ ≥ 12 ms for the same bytes) and would add round trips (㉘, not filed). (4) Beyond the bit-preserving sum (≈ 38–48 tok/s): 50 needs either a second reader — the CPU and the DSP reading at the same time, if the phone's memory lets two readers add up (target (b), #90) — or fewer bytes, which changes bits (track (c), #152, conditional on the resident path's accuracy). Earlier budget — where the 28 ms of a token went (cycle 16, from #120 A — estimate, derived from tok/s — not measured)

No run times a whole token piece by piece. LEDGER §2 estimates the split from two things measured in the same sitting: the token time of the tok/s binary (1000 / decode tok/s at G=512) and the level-2 [HTP-PROFILE] M==1 row of the G=64 profile run — for #120: A host 793.9 / dsp 711.7 / transport 82.2 µs per call, B host 799.0 / dsp 711.3 / transport 87.7, 22 calls per token. Everything the MoE calls do not account for is "outside the MoE call". Cycle 16 is the first time this arithmetic runs on a control cell of the feed default; it reproduces cycle 15's reading of the same code as a lever (#117 B: 27.40 = 15.7 + 2.0 + 9.7) within 0.6 ms, and the outside term — now read four times, on #117 A and B and on #120 A and B — sits at 9.7–10.3 ms whatever the MoE call does. Treat each number as ± a few percent: the profile row comes from a different run than the tok/s cells, and the transport column drifts between runs (rule 23).

What would move it (LEDGER's projections, not measurements) — the ladder after #120. (0) Done: the feed is the measured control (#120 A vs A0, +25.7 % at G=64; ㉒ closed). (1) One call per token (⑨): the main lever. Transport 1.8 + outside the call ≈ 10.3 = 12.1 ms against a ≈ 6.5 ms byte floor; ⑨ must deliver ≈ −5 ms — the 22 round trips (1.8), the CPU's FC / attention / norm / router / lm_head work between the calls (≈ 10.3, of which ⑰'s thread contention is ≈ 2–3), lm_head inside the DSP's op table (⑧). What has landed is the skeleton only: PR #119 (merged b4c96bdb, 2026-09-23; #85 closed) puts graph_init / graph_release / forward / forward_debug in the IDL, a validated 228-op description of the decode step (22 MoE ops) in htp_graph_desc.h, and a DSP-side op table in hexkl_graph.c whose only resident kind is MOE — so forward runs one MoE op and stops at the next non-resident op, and with NNTR_HTP_FORWARD=1 (off by default) it is still 22 calls per token: 0 ms by construction, no sitting ran it. The kernels that would make five more kinds resident landed on 2026-09-27 — PR #125 (#82, RMSNorm / q-k norm / RoPE / conv1d + gating at M=1), PR #126 (#81, m=1 attention with a DSP-resident KV cache), PR #127 (#84, the in-process host end-to-end harness that will gate the wiring) — but are not in the table; wiring them is #130, and plan 130 counts what that wiring gives on its own: 95 calls per token, not fewer, because every resident stretch is cut off from the MoE op by an FC, an ADD or the router that stay on the ARM (≈ +6–8 ms per token expected, off by default, a per-kind mask for a "MoE only" cell). And LEDGER's second ㉓ row says even those five are not enough: the call count falls only when every op between two MoE ops is resident — also the M=1 FC projections (conv in/out, q/k/v/o, the dense FFN of layers 0–1), the router + top-k, and the tail's final norm + lm_head — and those FC weights are Q4_0 on the NPU model, so it is a Q4_0 HVX GEMV on the DSP or a re-quantization that moves the weights against the CPU control (a user decision, like the accuracy column of the wiring handoff). (2) The in-situ engine gap (㉓): #120 A's ring runs at 32.3–34.4 GB/s where this unit's anchor reads 37.3 — ≈ 86 µs per call ≈ 1.9 ms per token, the last MoE-side lever on a 37 GB/s unit and nothing on a 31 GB/s one. The sitting that was to file it has now happened and shows the gap; LEDGER keeps it behind the ⑨ queue. Candidates: kick expert 0's gate_up before the activation quant, issue expert e+1's gate_up before expert e's down, deeper issue. (3) Bytes per token (§3.3): 22.02 MB per call is int4 already and VTCM holds ≈ 1.5 of the 4 experts, so only a smaller weight format moves the MoE floor; the ceiling itself moves only if the CPU and the DSP can read DDR at once — plan 90 (the two-reader probe, committed a7c66dec, p2) is the measurement that would say. Sum: MoE 13.0–15.5 + rest ≥ 6.5 = 19.5–22 ms → 45–51 tok/s. 50 sits at the physical ceiling (48–52) and is reachable only on a 37 GB/s unit with ⑨ at its floor; on a 31 GB/s unit the NPU ceiling is ≈ 45. LEDGER §2 budget rows (cycles 7, 9, 11, 12, 15 and 16), ㉒ and ㉔ rows, rules 23, 29, 34–35, §3 ⑧ ⑨ ⑰ ㉒ ㉓ (both rows); BENCHMARK.md "Goals" (NPU status cell, cycle 16); 120-upstream-4327-sync.md Results (means, level-2 rows); docs/plans/85-per-token-entry-skeleton.md §0–§1, §7; docs/plans/130-per-token-wiring.md §0–§1; LEDGER §3 ⑨ (cycle 16b); docs/plans/90-two-reader-ddr-probe.md §1; hmx/hexkl_graph.c:92–103 (the kernel table), htp_compute_ops.cpp:1162–1170

Cycle 19's reading against this budget (BENCHMARK "Goals", NPU status cell; not a new budget row — #130's A is a warm-phone cell). The ⑨ track is now measured on silicon. Its cheapest form, the entry with MOE alone resident (C), costs nothing: +6.8 / +5.1 / +0.3 % vs A, inside drift. Its wired form (B, all six kinds) costs +8.5 … +13.9 ms per token where the ladder above needs ≈ −5: the B-prof reads 95 × 220.1 µs = 20.9 ms of DSP time per token against A's 22 × 0.712 = 15.7, and the 73 extra round trips at 82–92 µs (rule 35) add ≈ 6–7 ms. Two terms must fall before ⑨ can pay: the call count — only ㉓'s larger resident set (#132: the M=1 FCs, the router + top-k, the final norm + lm_head) brings it below 22 — and the attention kernel's cost, ≈ 1.05 µs per KV position per layer (≈ 3.2 ms/token at pos 512, ≈ 7.1 at pos 1024; LEDGER ㉗), which #132's call-count fall does not remove. Neither is worth a sitting before B's text passes (#136), and no device cell of the switch is reproducible from a commit before #135. Consequences the ledger drew (rule 34): (a) #117's mm gate passes as measured here and would read ≈ 805–809 on a 31.2 GB/s unit (676.4 × 37.3 / 31.2); (b) a DMA-fed path's win is unit-dependent (931.9 / 676.4 = 1.38× here, ≈ 1.16× projected there, decode ≈ +10 % instead of +30 %) while a direct-read path's is not; (c) what transfers between units is the decode half of a gate, the mm half is scaled; (d) the goal's ceiling is per unit too — 484 MB of MoE weights per token is 13.0 ms at 37.3 GB/s and 15.5 at 31.2. 117-m1-gemv-vtcm-feed.md "Anchor (step 2)" and "Second sitting" (Anchor); BENCHMARK.md #117 side tables, first bullet; LEDGER rules 30, 32, 34

The same GEMV kernel over 22 MB of expert weights in three places: the ION arena (as in the model), a copy small enough to stay hot in L2 (the arithmetic alone), and now a vtcm cell where the DMA feed stages each expert into VTCM as in the layer call. Three readings. (1) The vtcm one-row cell reads 579 µs = 22 020 KB at 37.7 GB/s — the engine rate of this unit; 579 − 22 020 KB / 37.1 GB/s = −14.5 µs (against f2's 589.2: −10), so the feed has no measurable compute tail in the microbench and plan 117's §3.2 upgrade (lanes polling their slab's descriptor instead of one pool run per expert) is not triggered. (2) The lead-0 and lead-192 vtcm lines agree (579 / 579), as they must: the kernel forces the lead off under the feed. (3) The hot row reproduces #113's within 3 % and the arena row within 10 %, so the vector unit itself is the same on both phones. Sitting conditions and provenance

Two phones were attached (R3CN80CW3FY, an SM-N986N, and the S25 Ultra), so every command ran with ANDROID_SERIAL=R3CY10WM83Y. Battery 85 → 80 %, USB charging, screen off. Sitting 1: thermal_zone0 30 800 m°C at the cool start, 30 500 / 33 900 around the matrix, 30 800 before the profiles → 59 000 after, then 55 200 / 59 800 / 61 700 after the G = 64 / 512 / 1024 blocks; battery 28.3 → 39.5 °C. Sitting 2: battery 30.8 → 33.7 °C with the cool-down waits, zone0 still 55–60 °C during every E2E run — so most prefill cells sit below 497 for every variant alike, a warm phone's prefill, not a path change. Peak RSS 5 305 252 – 5 336 520 KB. No FARF/AEE errors. Provenance: the staged set under /local/mnt/workspace/htp_moe/117/, built in /home/j2z0-lee/nntrainer-117 @ 3c4912fa, ran as-is — md5sum -c md5.txt 0 mismatches, device md5sum of every pushed file = the table (logs/provenance.log, logs/skel.log): skel 5cea0a5a…, nntrainer_causallm 21221e90…, libnntrainer.so 39e375ad…, libcausallm_core.so ffbe4345…, libccapi-nntrainer.so d1bf42a3…, unittest_hvx_mm_u8i4 2781e9fb…, unittest_hvx_dma_probe 639c2eb4…. The four workstation sanity lines gave 0 / 0 / 1 / 0 (md5sum -c, no per-layer-type totals, one feed=%s source=%s, no staged libcdsprpc). One deviation: this phone had the NPU model under models/lfm2.5-8b-a1b-q40-qs4cx-wh as nntr_lfm2.5_8b_a1b_q40_arm.bin with another config (init_seq_len 1024, conv_block / dense_ffn / attn_proj_engine: htp); it was left untouched, models/q40-qs4cx-wh was created with the workstation's four config files plus the step-1 edits, and the .bin (md5 7b7867fa… = table) and tokenizer.json are symlinks into it — no 4.3 GB push. $D/libcdsprpc.so pre-existed on this phone with the md5 of /vendor/lib64/libcdsprpc.so, i.e. the system library, not the builddir stub rule 5 forbids; left in place. Sitting 2's logs are under $W/logs2/. A build trap the handoff records for the next person: build_android.sh --htp --cache skips the nntrainer builddir wholesale when libnntrainer.so already exists there, so an ARM-side edit is silently left out unless ninja -C builddir && ninja -C builddir install runs first (it bit #113 and the flip's restaging). 117-m1-gemv-vtcm-feed.md Results preamble (unit, thermal checkpoints), Artifacts (md5 table, the --cache trap), "Notes from the run", "Second sitting", E2E tables (peak RSS), "Landed defaults" (restaged set); BENCHMARK.md Artifacts (#117 row), #117 side tables "Thermal" and "Deviation"

Side tables from #113 (unit used R3CY205ZMND, 2026-09-23, one app set, one skel c78d1ef1… for all five cells, one gtest run)

The (loop × lead) matrix — MoeM1GemvFeedVsCompute, ns_per_tile, lower is better

The same GEMV kernel runs on three copies of the 22 MB of expert weights — in the ION arena (as in the model), in the DSP heap, and a set small enough to stay hot in the L2 cache — under both inner loops (rows1=0 four-row, rows1=1 one-row) and five l2fetch leads. One gtest invocation drives moe_set_opts itself and prints all 22 cells; inflight_kb = 2 × the lead (a lane holds the current block's boxes and the next block's; × 6 lanes for the L2 footprint).

celllead 0192 KB3847681536
arena, four-row (rows1=0)133.51282.34316.41323.08328.40
arena, one-row (rows1=1)154.34129.05139.37147.34155.23
hot, four-row72.1573.0771.6973.5373.07
hot, one-row42.7431.2531.7134.0132.63
heap123.44 (four-row)139.07 (one-row)———

Three readings. (1) The four-row loop is harmed by any lead, 2.1–2.5×, and the damage starts at the first non-zero value (192 KB, inflight_kb=384 ≈ 2.25 MB over six lanes) and then grows slowly — not a capacity cliff but interference: that loop already keeps eight vrmpy worth of loads in flight per quarter-tile, so it is not waiting on latency, and the prefetch box competes with its own loads for the same L2 and bus. The hot row proves it is not eviction: 71.7–73.5 throughout. (2) The one-row loop keeps the shape #105 measured: better to a minimum at 192 KB, worse past it, on both the arena (154.3 → 129.1 → 139.4 …) and the hot cell (42.7 → 31.3 → …). (3) The lead is an ordinal, not a byte count: the hardware allows three outstanding l2fetch per thread, so stage A issues the next block's gate box at the start of a block but its up box only after the last gate column — the up half gets about two column-computes of lead, whatever the nominal KB (a fourth outstanding box is #114's, now p2). Accuracy: bad_elems_M1 0 of 20480, bad_elems_M4 0 of 81920, bit_identical yes, no bad_cell_M* line, all ten (loop, lead) pairs compared; 22 path=m1_bench lines, no INVALID, [ PASSED ] 2 tests. 113-m1-gemv-lead-matrix.md "The matrix (step 3)" and its four paragraphs; BENCHMARK.md #113 side tables, first bullet; LEDGER rule 31; docs/plans/113-m1-gemv-lead-matrix.md §3

L* — the best four-row arena lead > 0 — came out as 192, the least-bad rather than a good value, and 192 is B, so the handoff's fallback clause sent C and D to 384; D192 was added (≈ 1 min) because that rule would otherwise have left the only cell with a chance at the gate unmeasured. Thermal: the profiles ran 27 500 → 57 700 m°C in issue order, so later cells are hotter; D192 was the hottest and still the fastest by 6 %, B and C were cooler and 2× slower — no ordering here is a heat artefact. Prefill gate: pass. The 16 replay cells (DMA_REPLAY_X, 20 calls each, all checksum_ok=y with tag=N/N), protocol run

Every cell pushes a descriptor list from the arena into VTCM with no compute between pushes; the f cells add an HVX job that streams VTCM at the same time (load=2). Lower µs per call and higher GB/s are better; depth_max is how many descriptors the ring had in flight at once.

Two readings. (1) Every validated cell sits at 30–32 GB/s whatever its descriptor count (8 / 24 / 28 / 36 / 46), chaining mode or destination: the engine itself tops out near 31 GB/s through any real per-call list on this day, and the shape question is moot. (2) load=2 costs 0.1–0.3 % over load=0 on all three feed shapes (699.8 → 700.5, 695.5 → 697.6, 700.5 → 701.8 µs): the vector unit streaming VTCM does not slow a DMA into it (rule 29). One-worker rows are 15–30 % low while four-worker rows are within 6 %, so whatever it is bites single-queue issue rate, not aggregate DDR bandwidth; a cooled repeat reproduces every #100 ratio within 1 %, so it is not this sitting's thermals. It matters because the feed verdict is absolute (≥ 37.0 GB/s) and the H0 verdict is not: scaled by 1.28, f2's load=2 would clear the gate. Hence the rule: any handoff with an absolute GB/s gate carries this cell as its in-sitting anchor, and #113's ride-along re-reads it first. This answers LEDGER ⑦'s open note in-sitting: the +≈ 100 µs #94 C and #105 showed survives PR #103's staging, is path cost, and buys 343 µs of DSP time per call — not a defect to chase; the 4.1 ms per token it costs returns only with one call per token. Sitting conditions and provenance

thermal_zone0 28 300 m°C before install → 34 900 after the first A cell → 59 300 after the six A cells → 46 500 at the gtest → 38 400 before A0 → 55 800 after the profiles and A r3 → 31 800 after a 300 s device-side cool-down (the repeat). Battery at 100 % on the charger throughout. Peak RSS 4 619 692 – 5 336 904 KB. A r3, run straight after A0 and both profiles at 55 800 m°C, read 16.71 tok/s (−40 % vs r1) with identical text — a thermal reading, not a regression; this unit throttles hard. Provenance: the three non-built files matched the handoff table exactly (libc++_shared.so b1586b9b…, libsdkl.so 0ad4e22a…, prompt512.txt fc65c158…), the model and tokenizer too; the skel and the five binaries were rebuilt from a8642bd1 (rule 22) and carry other md5s, as they must (rule 14). Feed or compute? — MoeM1GemvFeedVsCompute (device gtest on PR #107's branch, 22 MB of weights at m = 1)

The same kernel runs on three copies of the weights: in the ION arena (as in the model), in the ordinary DSP heap, and a set small enough to stay hot in the L2 cache. The hot cell is the arithmetic alone; the gap between arena and hot is the time spent waiting for bytes. Lower is better.

looparena ns/tileheap ns/tilehot ns/tilehot / arenaarena GB/sbit-identical (MoeLayerM1GemvMatchesHmx)
four-row (A)111.72115.7270.770.6326.5yes, 0 of 2048 / 0 of 8192 bad
one-row, lead 0 (B1)145.23140.1142.740.2920.9yes
one-row, lead 64 KB (B2)137.46145.4439.520.2922.1yes
one-row, lead 192 KB (B3)120.23133.2131.250.2625.0yes

Three readings: (1) hot ≪ arena in every variant, so the arena path is limited by the feed, not by instruction issue — under B3, (120.23 − 31.25) / 120.23 ≈ 74 % of mm is waiting (≈ 690 of 931 µs), ≈ 240 µs is arithmetic; under A's four-row loop the arithmetic is ≈ 590 µs. (2) The one-row loop is 2.26× faster hot (70.77 → 31.25), but with fewer loads in flight it hides DDR latency worse, so on the arena it is slower until the lead grows (145 → 137 → 120 ns/tile at 0 / 64 / 192 KB, not yet saturated). (3) heap ≈ arena within 11 %, so the ION mapping is not the problem: this is the plain 21–27 GB/s a vector unit gets reading DDR directly (rule 27). [ PASSED ] 2 tests. under all four skels. This microbench lives on PR #107's branch; it is not in htp_moe's gtest yet. 105-m1-gemv-compute.md "Gtests", "What the microbench says"; LEDGER ㉒ row, rules 26–27

Wall-2 nomination was row h — and #100 dissolved it. As #94 read it: the in-situ engine rate while it runs is 21.9–32.3 GB/s; the same unit's isolated probe of the identical per-descriptor shape does 106.9 GB/s; the replay of the real list with nothing else running sits in between at 40.2. So rule 11's "4–7×" was split into ≈ 2.7× inside the descriptor list / region layout itself (h) and ≈ 1.3–1.8× in the interleaving with compute (f + g ≈ 20 % of dsp_us). #100 then showed the 106.9 (and 73.2 a day later) is a number no real per-call path can reproduce: the validated per-call ceiling is 26.3 GB/s and the list beats it. What survives of the #94 reading: the paced replay reproduces the in-situ 15.6–18.6 GB/s (the interleaving, rows f + g, is real), more workers make the replay slower, and the engine tops out near 31 GB/s through any real list. Rows f and g are not filed: with the GEMV the default, the ring is off the decode path. What each measurement decided

#234 P4 — the FC WH sidecar on the one-PD token: +8.4–9.0 % on the 26B dummy, bytes not rate are next; an accuracy flag sends the FC source format to the user (2026-10-08 10:26–11:00 KST, unit used R3CY205ZMND attached to the workstation, agent adb; skel v79 5cdd307e…, SDK 6.4.0.1; 234-p4-fcwh-decode.md @ 1497bee56, PR #257; folded in cycle 42)

S5-0 had found the one-PD token ≈ 30 % under the hybrid because the DSP read the FC, dense and head weights as Q4_0 through its CPU-exact kernel at ≈ 43 GB/s. P4 asked how much of that a copy of the FC and dense weights in the DSP's own layout removes. It also asked whether the 26B loads with the original config.json (#253's device confirmation) and whether the tokens stay the hybrid's. Measured: F16 12.36 / 15.89–15.90 / 14.86 against E16 11.34 / 14.61–14.68 / 13.65 (+9.0 / +8.4 / +8.9 %) and A16 19.24 / 18.37 / 17.40. The DSP wall fell 61.82 → 56.25 ms (FC −3.67, DENSE_FFN −1.99). Mapped memory fell 2048 → 1984 MiB; peak RSS did not move (2142–2165), because the sidecar lives in rpcmem and the ARM process still holds its ≈ 880 MiB of Q4_0 FCs for the prefill. All 13 runs loaded the original config.json. Read as: lever cells with a flag, not rows of record (the PR was open at the fold; the sidecar is a re-quantization); the Gemma "now" stays S5-0's. What it moves: (1) rate is nearly spent (49.4 MiB/ms against ≈ 57 GB/s), so the next lever is bytes: #229 S2, 2-bit FCs + dense (plan 229 §8: −8.0 ms). The LM_HEAD at 4 bits on WH would buy ≈ 1 ms; at 2 bits ≈ 4.6, but only if the tied embedding is ternary. (2) Rule 73: a sidecar re-quantized from an already-quantized FC is a second quantization, so its host SNR gate needs an int4-exact fixture (gemma64x: 30.80 dB against the Q4M1 token, 8 / 8 tokens, a gate/up-swap mutant −1.76 dB). On plain gemma64 re-quantizing twice costs −4 dB, and the WH path's u8 rounding of each input row costs 9.5 dB on the sliding q / k projections under a peaked softmax. (3) ㊸: the real checkpoint hits the same stacking if its FCs arrive as Q4_0. The user chose option A: the producer writes the attention and dense FCs per column as QS4CX, so the sidecar becomes a lossless repack (plan 229 §8.2; the code note on page 2 §2.9 adds that the codes, not f32 values, must reach the file). (4) Rule 72's 4096 config copy is retired. Not run: a CPU-only cell, prompt 512, second runs at G 64 / 1024, the PPL / dump gate at G 512, any separation of the two accuracy causes (needs a source-written sidecar → #258). BENCHMARK Method (cycle-42 paragraph), Results (P4 ×3), side tables; LEDGER cycle 42 (1)–(7), rules 72–73, §2 #234 P4 row, §3 ㉘ ㉟ ㊸; plan 229 §8; the cycle-43 brief (option A)

#250 / PR #251 — the external int2 file becomes QS2CX_WH: the repack tool is ours (code, no sitting; merged 2026-10-07 as 24a42c19f)

The user's 26B file (a dummy now; the real checkpoint will come from the same script and settings) stores the 2-bit experts almost as QS2CX_WH but not quite: each code sits at tile byte sl/4 — so a byte's two halves feed neighbouring int4 bytes — and the file has no palette, a weight being code − 2. tools/htp/repack_int2_to_qs2cx.py moves every code to whCodeByte2's place (a byte's halves then feed int4 bytes 128 apart), inserts the palette fe ff 00 01 = {−2, −1, 0, +1} so no code value changes, and copies scales, column sums and every other tensor byte for byte; repack_int2_check.cc then checks every byte (REPACK CHECK experts=7680 elements=22837985280 mismatches=0 ok; 7 226 112 120 → 7 226 142 840 B; 35 s). What the check cannot see: the input decode is the layout assumption itself, and a column sum does not notice rows reordered inside a column — only a model output against a reference catches that, which S5-0 then supplied. With it the user closed #229 S0 (format pinned: experts 2-bit in 32 × 32 WH tiles, per-column fp32 scale + column sum, values {−2, −1, 0, +1}, everything else Q4_0) and set moe_layer_dtype to QS2CX_WH. Explained with a diagram on page 2 §2.8. plan 250; repack_int2_to_qs2cx.py header; commits 1391ae9e2, 1dfe72182; contract §1 Model row, §12 (2026-10-07); BENCHMARK Artifacts (the two bin rows)

#219 — a pool miss served from a cached ARM tier: +19–24 % at G = 64 on the E2E path, the one-thread copy is the faster one, gate G2 fails; the default is the user's (2026-10-06 18:28–19:12 KST, unit used R3CY205ZMND on the device farm, S25 v79, run by the user; set = local merge 8629b9392, skel v79 44339c7a…, SDK 6.4.0.1 per the handoff's Artifacts header; 219-arm-tier.md @ 149481ec4; folded in cycle 36)

On the one-PD E2E path only 28 of each layer's 32 experts fit in the DSP's arena; a token that needs one of the other four misses and the expert is fetched. Until now a miss read the model file through Android's page cache, which the kernel kept evicting under the arena's memory pressure, so a miss cost 2–8 ms and varied run to run. #219 (PR #224, on htp_first_version) keeps the 88 non-resident experts (466.5 MiB) as copies in ordinary cached ARM memory, so a miss is a memory copy into the arena slot; a refill thread reads replaced entries back with O_DIRECT, off the token path. Measured: the G = 64 block went 39.99 → 47.60 (fresh boot, +19.0 %) and 38.70 → 47.82 (old boot, +23.6 %); G 512 / 1024 +1.4–4.3 % to 52.3–52.8; a miss 2.05–4.13 → 0.51–0.83 ms; file refaults 63k–153k → ≤ 571; kswapd scanning → 0. Every text equals A; the prefill gate passes on block means; the hybrid is unchanged. Failed: G2 — the refill does not stay ahead of the next misses at decode start (tier_waits 3–4 a run, gate ≤ 2) and the phone still swapped pages in (pswpin 0–2105, gate 0); G1 (≤ 0.5 ms a miss) passes only with =2. Decided: rule 65 — the copy into an uncached ION slot is store-bound and one writer is faster than eight (11.8–12.3 GB/s vs 6.4–10.4); a tier-style lever is gated on its wait count, not only on ms a miss. #216 lever — NNTR_MOE_FADVISE=1 makes every miss a cache hit and costs the prefill; not the default (2026-10-01 23:06–23:36 KST, unit used R3CY10WM83Y, run by an agent, 216-fadvise.md @ 45be8de65 on origin/htp/216-fadvise, PR #218 merged env-only on 2026-10-02 as 238a280b7, default off; folded in cycle 30)

Step 1 (below) had shown that a slow miss is the model file read back from storage after the kernel evicted its pages. The lever asks the kernel to drop an expert's file pages once its bytes sit in the ION slot (DONTNEED) and to read the evicted victim back into the page cache (WILLNEED), in 128 KiB pieces on one worker thread. On a fresh and an old boot, Q28 one PD, A / B interleaved four times at G = 64: decode block means 36.27 → 41.56 (+14.6 %) and 38.67 → 42.71 (+10.5 %); ms per miss 0.86–1.13 on all ten B runs against A's 0.68–4.38 — the bimodal miss becomes a constant one; at G = 512 (one profiled run each) 45.77 → 45.52 and 46.06 → 48.05. Text 26 / 26 identical to sitting 1's A, calls/token=1.00. But posix_fadvise is not a hint on this kernel (6.6.77-android15): each call costs 2–10 ms of the caller's time (rule 62), and beside the 8-thread prefill that is −7 to −18 % prefill — the prefill gate fails, and no placement tried removed it. Verdict: a lever cell, env-only on PR #218, default not flipped (the user decides at review); the next lever avoids the syscalls on both paths — a user-space copy of the complement (#219). 216-fadvise.md A/B, Gate, Reading; BENCHMARK Results (#216 lever row), Method (cycle-27 paragraph, cycle-30 sentence), Log cycle 30; LEDGER rule 62, ㉜

#216 step 1 — the slow pool miss is a storage read under page-cache eviction, not a busy core and not boot proximity (2026-10-01 21:43–22:01 KST, unit used R3CY10WM83Y, 216-miss-read.md on origin/htp/216-miss-read, PR #217; folded in cycle 30)

Twelve profiled Q28 G = 64 runs on three boots, with a phone-side sampler (per-core load, vmstat, PSI) beside each. ms per miss ranged 0.55–5.77; the busiest non-app core was 3–16 % busy in 11 of 12 windows, slow and fast alike (hypothesis "a busy core stalls the 8-slice read" refuted). On the boot where the counters were sampled, ms/miss 5.03 / 2.72 / 1.55 / 0.55 went with 610 / 310 / 61 / 0 MiB read from storage (pgpgin) in the decode window, and every slow window carried 92–296 ms of PSI io stall. The 3.3 GB ION arena, the 4.1 GB model file and the rest of Android share 11.1 GB, so kswapd evicts the file's pages during the run and a re-missed expert comes from UFS. Slow runs also happened at uptime 300 s and after a 6-minute idle, so rule 61's "wait 5 minutes after a boot" was a correlation of one sitting; rule 61 was amended: a pool-miss number is readable only with the window's pgpgin or PSI io next to it. No tok/s row of record (profiled runs). 216-miss-read.md table and Reading; BENCHMARK Results (#216 step 1 row); LEDGER rule 61 (amended), ㉜

#211 — the two-PD E2E path is removed; one PD is the only E2E entry (code, 2026-10-01, PR #215 merged as aa78104e9; no sitting)

After rule 59 the user decided that the second DSP session goes. NNTR_HTP_E2E=1 now always means one PD (the FC set, lm_head and the expert pool on session 1's arena), and a new E2E row is labelled Q<C> (NNTR_HTP_E2E=1 NNTR_MOE_CACHE_EXPERTS=<C>). All-resident E0 no longer loads on the 8B model (3696 MiB of experts + 448 MiB of FC set > the PD's 3840; the load error names NNTR_MOE_CACHE_EXPERTS), and NNTR_HTP_E2E_PDS other than 1 is refused at load. The E0 and P rows below stay as history. #149 step 0 — the feed engine has no schedule lever; ㉓ closed with no code (2026-09-28 20:56–20:58, unit used R3CY10WM83Y, issue comment, folded in cycle 21)

#136 — the six-kind text flip is quantizer amplification, not a defect; the pool race is real but not the cause (2026-09-28 16:37–17:00, unit used R3CY10WM83Y, 136-forward-text.md, folded at cycle 20 close)

Plan 136 found a real race on the host — a worker could join the next job of the DSP's worker pool uncounted and let the pool return while it still wrote — and PR #143 fixed it (two job slots indexed by generation, an acquire re-check; the 30-way concurrent host E2E went 25/30 → 30/30). On silicon it left the six-kind text the #130 loop word for word. The bisect over the executable masks then showed every kind group alone and every pair text-identical except RMSNORM together with ROPE + ATTN_M1; 71 and 89 calls pass where 77 fails, so neither the call count nor CONV1D_GATE is involved. The dumps said why: switch-off and KINDS=MOE are bit-identical run to run, a resident RMSNORM alone moves the first decode token's MoE inputs to 34 → 18 dB and the attention stretch alone to 40 → 18 dB, each recovering at token 2 — and the two stacked flip the token at pos 512. Recomputing every dumped RMSNORM input in the Android CPU's order put the DSP's output at 131–148 dB from it, and nearer f64. Decided (rule 39): text identity cannot hold for any resident kind on this quantized pipeline — the CPU's Q4_0 activation quantizer and the MoE's u8 quantizer flip a level wherever a value sits on a rounding boundary; the gate for such variants becomes decode PPL vs A + text + approval; per-kind exactness is checked by recomputing dumped stretch inputs (NNTR_HTP_DUMP_ALL); rule 37's "logic / binding fault" reading is withdrawn. Side number: switch-off 36.61 tok/s at G=64, one run on a cool phone — a note. 136-forward-text.md; LEDGER §2 #136 row, rules 37 (amended), 39, §3 ㉕ (closed); plan 136; PR #143

PR #119 — the per-token entry skeleton lands (merged 2026-09-23 as b4c96bdb, #85 closed; no sitting of its own)

Not a measurement, recorded here because it changes the tree every later sitting builds from and because its own claim is "0 ms". Plan 85's skeleton for one call per token: the IDL gains graph_init / graph_release / forward / forward_debug after moe_set_opts; htp_backend/htp_graph_desc.h defines the decode step as a list of ops in plain 32-bit words (header, per-layer kinds, one 76-word record per op with its kind, shapes, activation slots, next weight-streaming op, and for a MoE op its 32 + 32 registered weight handles), a validator that returns one named AEE_* code per finding and never AEE_EBADPARM (so that code stays the stale-skel symptom, rule 3), and the LFM2 builder, which for LFM2.5-8B-A1B emits 228 ops, 22 of them MoE; hmx/hexkl_graph.c keeps the validated table on the DSP and runs forward as "from the start op, while the op is resident, call its kernel; report where you stopped", bracketing every op in HAP_perf_get_pcycles and printing one FARF line per call — and its kernel table has one non-NULL slot, MOE, which calls the existing hexkl_mm_u8i4_moe_layer_run at M = 1 unchanged. On the ARM, ComputeOps::set_decode_graph_desc(words) is one core virtual; lfm2_moe_causallm.cpp builds the description when NNTR_HTP_FORWARD=1 and moe_engine is htp; htp_compute_ops.cpp validates it with the same validator before any FastRPC call, binds each layer's handles to the next MoE op in list order during the first pass, calls graph_init once all 22 are bound, and from then on sends every M == 1 MoE call through forward (or forward_debug under NNTR_HTP_PROFILE ≥ 2) with the same staging pools and profile bucket as the per-layer call; it throws if resume_at ≠ op + 1, and errors never fall back to the per-layer entry (a fallback would report per-layer numbers as the graph's). graph_host_check joins run_host_checks.sh (the LFM2.5 list validates and each mutation fails with its own code; forward with nothing resident is the identity; with MOE resident it is byte-equal to a direct layer_run). What it buys today: nothing. Resident = MOE only, so forward runs exactly one op per call and it is still 22 calls per token (plan 85 §0: "0 ms by construction"); the per-call arguments shrink (the 64 handles no longer travel), but no sitting has measured the switch on, and the default path is byte-for-byte the pre-#119 one. The ride-along for the next sitting: one decode token with the switch on, expecting [HTP] graph: init n_ops=228 resident=MOE moe_ops=22 and text ≡ switch-off at G=64; 0x8000040E from the graph entries means a skel built before this PR. #117 — the VTCM feed passes its gate twice; the feed becomes the default; the DMA bound turns out to be per unit (2026-09-23, unit used R3CY10WM83Y, filled at 6412bfc9, folded at 63a20f69; landed defaults recorded at 1182cb59)

After #113 the M=1 GEMV's compute side was spent and the one thing left inside the MoE call was the rate at which the expert weights reach the vector unit: 23.5 GB/s for the direct arena read, against a DMA engine that #100 had measured at 31.6 GB/s beside a busy HVX. #117 built the feed the way ㉒ had described it — each expert's gate/up and down matrices as one whole-matrix descriptor each (the f2 shape), issued one expert ahead into two 3.5 MiB VTCM slabs that alternate by expert (the down matrices land two per slab once a slab has no gate/up left to hold), one pool run per expert and stage so that every slab reuse follows a join, a host scoreboard that checks that schedule exactly, and the l2fetch lead forced off because a prefetch of a VTCM address is meaningless. It rode as bit 17 of the moe_set_opts word (bit 5 = authoritative), read from a fourth variable, NNTR_MOE_HTP_GEMV_FEED, so one skel and one app served A (D192, arena), A0 (the pre-#115 cell — #113's device confirmation) and B (the feed). The gate had three parts and all three passed, in a warm sitting and again after the phone was cooled: level-2 M==1 mm 676.6 / 676.4 µs against 922.7 / 931.9 (gate ≤ 760), decode B ≥ A at all three G (+25.2 / +35.7 / +23.2 %, then +32.5 / +30.9 / +28.6 %), text byte-identical in 36 of 36 runs; plus bit_identical yes over ten (loop, lead) pairs × {arena, feed}, the in-situ engine at 32.3–34.4 GB/s (gate ≥ 28), and prefill untouched (M>1 dsp ±0.04 %; the −8.5 % prefill cell at G=1024 in sitting 2 is A's own pair, rule 27). Two things the sitting taught beyond its gate. First, the round trip fell too: transport 190 → 92 µs per call, the HMX path's level — so the "+100 µs GEMV path cost" that #100 had booked and the ledger had said "comes back only with one call per token" was the direct arena read's, and ≈ 2.2 ms per token came back with the feed (rule 35; cause not separated). Second, the anchor moved: this is the user's second S25 Ultra, and its untouched DMA cell read 607.9 / 605.3 µs (37.1 / 37.3 GB/s) where R3CY205ZMND reads 724.0 (31.2) — while D192's mm, the HVX read of DDR, is the same on both (922.7 / 931.9 vs 937.0). The difference is in the DMA engine or its clock, not in DDR or HVX, and three anchor values now exist (40.2 once, 31.2–31.4 twice, 37.1–37.3 twice). Rule 34 draws the consequences: an absolute mm gate on a DMA-fed path is read next to the sitting's anchor (676.4 passes here and would read ≈ 805–809 there), a DMA-fed path's win is unit-dependent (≈ +30 % here, ≈ +10 % projected there) while a direct-read path's is not, and the goal's ceiling is per unit too (484 MB of MoE weights = 13.0 ms at 37.3 GB/s, 15.5 at 31.2). The supervisor's verdict (cycle 15): gate passed, the feed lands by the gate this time, not by rule 33. PR #118 flipped HVX_GEMV_M1_FEED to 1u and made the ARM send the feed bit explicitly whenever the env names nothing, so an unset run prints [HTP] moe m1 gemv: on (applied=0x303e1) lead=192KB rows1=1 feed=vtcm source=default; NNTR_MOE_HTP_GEMV_FEED=0 is the opt-out (0x103e1 feed=arena = this sitting's A, the next sitting's A0), and LEAD_KB=0 ROWS1=0 FEED=0 the pre-#115 cell (0xe1). Merged as d79c0efe. BENCHMARK's "now" moved to this sitting's A (28.22 / 27.89 / 27.23 — the first control that is the D192 default, as rule 33 required), and stayed there until the flipped default was measured as its own sitting's A — which #120 did the same evening (35.97 / 35.96 / 35.17, above); B's 37 tok/s was a lever cell until then. #114 closed (moot: no lead form has anything left to prefetch under the feed; the vtcm microbench cell runs at the engine rate with a −14.5 µs tail). The residual named ㉓ — the in-situ engine 32–34 vs the anchor's 37.3, ≈ 86 µs per call ≈ 1.9 ms per token — is not an issue yet; it is filed after the flip's own sitting shows the gap on the unit that runs it. The per-token budget was re-derived on both A and B (35.86 → 27.40 ms at G=512; MoE dsp 15.7, transport 2.0, outside 9.7), and the order from here is: land the feed as the measured control → ⑨ (#85 → #82 → #81; ≈ −5 ms needed) → ㉓ if the gap shows. 117-m1-gemv-vtcm-feed.md Why, Variants, "The gate, as read", Results (all five subsections), "Second sitting", Verdict, Notes, "Landed defaults (after the sitting)"; docs/plans/117-m1-gemv-vtcm-feed.md §0–§5; LEDGER §2 ㉒ feed-half row and the cycle-15 budget row, rules 27, 29, 33–35, §3 ⑦ ⑨ ㉒ ㉓; BENCHMARK.md Method, Goals, Results, #117 side tables, Artifacts, Log cycle 15; hexkl_mm_u8i4_moe.c:440–466, hexkl_mm_u8i4_moe.h:185–186, htp_moe_opts.h:46–49, 79–88, htp_compute_ops.cpp:1157–1170; contract §1; issues #113 #114 #117, PRs #115 #118

#113 — the lead helps only the loop that needs it; D192 lands by decision, the feed half closes at 31 GB/s (2026-09-23, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at d03c8929 / 37fffcb4, folded at 861131dc)

After #100 the plan was "compute-side wins first": #105 had shown the M=1 GEMV waiting on DDR latency (≈ 74 % of mm under the one-row loop) with one knob measured against it, the l2fetch lead, still improving at 192 KB — and #105 had confounded two axes, because its A was the four-row loop with no lead and its B cells the one-row loop with three leads, so the loop the phone runs by default had never had a lead. #113 separated them: PR #115 (absorbing PR #107) put the loop and the lead into spare bits of the moe_set_opts word — bit 7 says bits 15:8 (the lead in 64 KB units) are authoritative, bit 6 says bit 16 (rows1) is — and made the ARM throw if the skel's echo differs, so one skel and one app cover every cell and a stale skel cannot run the wrong one. The gtest printed the whole 2 × 5 matrix in one run, and the hypothesis came back inverted: the four-row loop with any lead is 2.1–2.5× slower on the arena and unchanged on the hot cell, damage starting at the first non-zero lead — interference with its own loads, not eviction (rule 31); the one-row loop improves to 192 KB and degrades past it, exactly #105's shape. In the layer call: B (four-row, 192 KB) mm +92.5 %, C (four-row, 384) +112.6 %, D (one-row, 384) +4.3 %, and the cell the handoff's L* rule would have skipped, D192 (one-row, 192 KB), −6.3 % at 937.0 µs — the same number #105 B3 gave (930.9). The gate, mm ≤ 840 on at least one variant, failed on all five, and the matrix leaves nothing unmeasured that could pass. Accuracy passed (bit-identical over all ten pairs), prefill passed (M>1 dsp within 0.08 %). With the verdict already fixed by step 4, the E2E block was spent on the one live question, whether the matrix minimum is an end-to-end win: A vs D192 mirrored at all three G read +4.11 / +3.29 / +4.26 %, text byte-identical 6/6, prefill −0.77 / +0.05 / +3.73 % — the first decode gain any GEMV variant has produced across #100, #105 and #113. The sitting's own verdict was "close PR #107, keep the machinery, defaults unchanged"; the user overrode it (2026-09-23, "1번으로 진행", rule 33): a sub-gate win may land as the default when it is consistent at all three G in one sitting, text-identical and prefill-clean, while the issue's gate still says whether the issue is done. So PR #115 flips HVX_GEMV_M1_ROWS1 to 1u and HVX_GEMV_PF_LEAD_KB to 192u, an unset run prints [HTP] moe m1 gemv: on (applied=0x103c1) lead=192KB rows1=1 source=default, and NNTR_MOE_HTP_GEMV_LEAD_KB=0 NNTR_MOE_HTP_GEMV_ROWS1=0 still reaches the old cell (applied=0xc1) for the next sitting's A/A0. No new handoff: the device confirmation rides the next sitting (#117's or whichever comes first), and BENCHMARK's "now" stays #100 A until then. The ride-along closed rule 30: the untouched DMA cell read 724.0 µs / 31.2 GB/s cold, #100's value not #94's, so 31–32 GB/s is this unit's real single-queue DMA bound (rule 32) and ㉒'s feed half closes as a 37 GB/s question — and re-files as a rate lever, #117 (p1): stage each expert's weights into VTCM with #100's f2 shape under the one-row loop, 31.6 vs 23.5 GB/s ≈ 1.33×, gate mm ≤ 760, the l2fetch lead off when the feed is on. #105 and #100 closed (no compute-side headroom left under a direct feed; nothing left in ⑥), PR #107 closed, #114 (the up-half lead) dropped to p2 as moot if the feed lands. The per-token budget for the pending default is ≈ 35.5 ms at G=512 (cycle 12), and the track after this cycle is ⑨: #85 → #82 → #81, the ≈ 14 ms of transport and ARM work outside the MoE call. 113-m1-gemv-lead-matrix.md Why, Variants, Results (all five subsections), Verdict, Notes, "Landed defaults (after the sitting)"; docs/plans/113-m1-gemv-lead-matrix.md §1–§4; LEDGER §2 ㉒ row (cycle 12), cycle-12 budget row, rules 31–33, §3 ⑥ ㉒; BENCHMARK.md Goals (Method note on the pending default), Results, #113 side tables, Log cycle 12; docs/plans/117-m1-gemv-vtcm-feed.md §0–§1; issues #100 #105 #113 #114 #117, PRs #107 #115

#100 — row h dissolves, the VTCM feed is demoted, the GEMV default is confirmed on a device (2026-09-23, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at 4bf1fdac, folded at b4002d36)

Two questions and a ride-along. Row h's cause. #94 had found the old kernel path's 46-descriptor DMA list replaying at 40 GB/s where an isolated probe of the same shape read 107, and named that 2.7× "the list's own loss" (rule 19). Plan 100 wrote down five hypotheses and a sixth, H0: the probe is not a ceiling. To test it, PR #112 added sixteen replay cells to the gtest, each with its destination bytes summed and checked after the run, among them c_star — the probe's own per-call shape, one descriptor at a time, contents verified. In one log c_star read 26.3 GB/s, 0.36× the same run's probe, and the traced list 1.19× c_star: H0 fires alone (H1 0.41, the list row 0.99), the real list is faster than any rate the engine delivered through a checked per-call path, and every checked cell sits at 30–32 GB/s whatever its descriptor count, chaining or destination. So row h dissolves, rule 19 is withdrawn, rule 11 keeps only its useful half ("wall 2 is decided by instrumentation inside the call, never by a probe"), and rows f and g are not filed because the GEMV default has taken the ring off the decode path (rule 28, LEDGER ⑥ closed as a list question). The feed shape. The gate asked for a DDR → VTCM shape at ≥ 80 % of c_star and ≥ 37.0 GB/s while HVX streams VTCM. The best, f2, passed the first half (1.21) and failed the second (31.6), with HVX costing only 0.1–0.3 % (rule 29). But the one cell PR #112 did not touch, DMA_REPLAY workers=1 load=0 pace=0, read 561.5 µs per call in #94 and 719.6 here on the same unit — 1.28× slower, not thermal (a cooled repeat reproduces every ratio within 1 %), biting single-queue issue rate rather than aggregate bandwidth, cause not separated (session clock/governor vs the rebuilt skel; rule 30). Scaled by it, f2 would clear 37.0 — hence the user's own reading, "do not close ㉒'s feed half on this sitting alone". What the number does settle: even at its best the DMA feed is ≈ 1.2× the GEMV's direct read (21–27 GB/s), not the 1.6× cycle 9 assumed, and it costs a ring and a double buffer. The user's direction: stop leaning on DMA micro-numbers, take the compute-side wins first — the l2fetch lead (#113, p0; #114 for the cross-expert kick), with the feed half open but unfiled behind #113's ride-along (re-read the untouched cell; at 40.2 GB/s it re-opens) — #113 read it the same day: 724.0 µs / 31.2 GB/s, so it did not re-open and the drift is persistent, rule 32). The ride-along (PR #108). A, the merged default, ran 27.85 / 27.09 / 26.45 tok/s and became the "now"; A0, the same binaries with NNTR_MOE_HTP_M1_GEMV=0, read 24.58 at G=64 — A is +13.5 % (the handoff expected ≈ 6 %), text identical once the banner line is excluded, so PR #108's default is a measured win, not an inherited one. The level-2 rows answered LEDGER ⑦'s open note in-sitting: transport GEMV 185.8 vs HMX 84.6 µs per call with equal staging classes, i.e. the +101 µs is path cost and buys 343 µs of dsp; not chased. ㉑'s print fix works on silicon (rest 0.8 % of host, 5.76 lanes busy over mm). The per-token budget was re-estimated (36.9 ms at G=512: MoE dsp 22.9 / transport 4.1 / outside ≈ 9.9) and the ladder re-ordered: lead → (feed) → one call per token. A hot re-run at the end (55.8 °C) read 16.71 tok/s, −40 %, text identical: this unit throttles hard, and it is the reason every sitting mirrors its order and logs thermal_zone0. 100-dma-chunk-list.md Why, Verdict, "Session drift", E2E and profile tables, gtest table, Notes; docs/plans/100-dma-chunk-list.md §0, §3.2–3.3; LEDGER §2 ⑥ row, #101 ride-along row, cycle-11 budget row, rules 28–30, 11, 19, §3 ⑥ ⑦ ㉑ ㉒ (cycle 11); BENCHMARK.md Goals, #100 side tables; issues #100 #113 #114; PRs #112 #115

#105 — the GEMV is waiting for its weights, not computing (2026-09-22, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at da41340b)

The question was whether the M=1 GEMV's 973 µs per call could be cut on the arithmetic side. Plan 105 had read the compiled loop and found that at one row it still ran the four-row loop (32 vrmpy multiply-accumulates per weight tile, 8 of them useful) and fetched each column into the cache only right before use. PR #107 replaced it with a one-row loop and added a prefetch lead, measured as B1 (no lead), B2 (64 KB, the branch default) and B3 (192 KB). The gate — mm more than 5 % below A and decode ≥ A at every length — failed for all three. B1 made mm 7.4 % worse and decode 4–8 % worse; B2 +3.3 % and −2 to −6 %; B3 −4.3 % (inside the ±5 % band) and +2.6 / +2.2 / −1.7 %. Nothing came near the ≤ 600 µs that closes LEDGER ㉒. Accuracy passed everywhere (bit-identical under all four skels, text = A in 18 of 18 cells) and prefill passed on the M>1 DSP time. The value of the sitting is the microbench: at m = 1 the GEMV over the arena is bound by DDR read latency, not by instruction issue (rule 26), and ION vs heap makes no difference (rule 27), so ≈ 74 % of mm under the one-row loop is feed. That turned the plan around: the lever is to stream each expert's weights into VTCM by DMA while the vector unit computes, and the one-row loop is what lets that feed hide the arithmetic (≈ 240 µs left instead of ≈ 590). PR #107 is held, not merged: merged as-is its 64 KB default is a measured regression, and once weights stream from VTCM the prefetch lead has nothing to do. The future feed issue rebases onto it; if #100 cannot certify a feed shape, the fallback is to land #107 with a lead of ≥ 192 KB after a sweep. #100 was re-scoped to exactly that certification and raised to p0; #105 went to review, p1, waiting on it. A's decode, 27.74 / 26.88 / 24.83 tok/s, became the NPU "now" (distance 1.86× at G=512), because it is the path PR #108 makes the default; #100 A replaced it a day later, reproducing it within +0.4 / +0.8 / +6.5 %. A side reading for LEDGER ⑦: the GEMV path's transport was 182–184 µs per call here against 87.9 on the HMX path in #88, the same +≈ 95 µs #94 C showed before PR #103 — cross-sitting, so the next sitting reads it as an A/A0 (101-ride-along.md R2/R4). Rebuilding on a fresh checkout hit four gaps the recipes did not state (empty subprojects/, the per-checkout tokenizer library, libc++_shared.so's location, HEXKL_ROOT); they are now in the gates skill and on page 1. 105-m1-gemv-compute.md Verdict, microbench, Follow-ups, Notes; LEDGER §2 ㉒ row and cycle-9 budget row, rules 26–27, §3 ⑥ ⑦ ㉒, §3a; BENCHMARK.md Log cycle 9; issues #100 #105

PR #108 — the GEMV path becomes the default (merged 2026-09-22 as 8e121dbe, no sitting of its own)

#101 and #102 in one PR, on the ARM side only (libnntrainer.so; no skel, IDL or DSP change). With NNTR_MOE_HTP_M1_GEMV unset every decode MoE call now takes the HVX GEMV path; =0 opts out. The run proves its path with one line, [HTP] moe m1 gemv: on (applied=0x1) source=default (or source=env when the variable was set). The rule is therefore inverted from the sitting-2 days: from now on an A log that says off or lacks source=default is the old HMX path and voids the sitting as a reference. A skel older than moe_set_opts now fails the first MoE call unless =0 is set, on purpose. The level-2 profile row of the GEMV path was fixed: swiglu there is the sum of lane time, so it is no longer subtracted from rest, and a weight DMA: n/a (direct arena read inside mm, no ring; swiglu = lane-time, N.NN lanes busy over mm) line takes the missing line's place. A host check pins both (MOE M1 GEMV OPTS: unset=on 0=off 1=on, MOE PROFILE ROW: gemv rest>=0 hmx unchanged). Its four ride-along cells (R1 proof-line count on every A log, R2 A's level-2 row, R3 an A0 opt-out tok/s cell expected ≈ 6 % below A with identical text, R4 A0's level-2 row, whose transport difference with R2 answers the ⑦ question) rode #100's handoff and all held on 2026-09-23: R1 = 1 in every A log, R3 read A +13.5 % (more than expected) with identical text, R2/R4 gave transport 185.8 vs 84.6 (see the #100 paragraph above). docs/measurements/101-ride-along.md; htp_compute_ops.cpp:1102–1143 (sendMoeOptsOnce), :640–655; htp_moe_opts.h; test/htp/host/moe_opts_host_check.c:38, 56; LEDGER ⑯ ㉑, the first rule 24; BENCHMARK.md Log cycle 10; 100-dma-chunk-list.md E2E table and Profiles

#95 — Hadamard rotation: an accuracy win at no measurable cost, off-tree (2026-09-22 16:10–17:05 KST, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at 3a566761)

Not a speed item and not on htp_moe: the sitting ran on the side tree htp_hadamard (upstream 3006d255 plus tooling: HMX loop only, no GEMV, no DMA trace), so BENCHMARK.md carries no row for it and nothing here is compared with the htp_moe rows. The change folds a Hadamard rotation into the down-projection weights offline and undoes it on the DSP right before the u8 requant, to spread rounding error. Result: perplexity (PPL, how surprised the model is by the prompt; lower is better) 115.1 on A → 100.5 with the rotation (−12.7 %), below the CPU model's 109.8 — the first NPU variant under the CPU; requant SNR median +8.0 dB over ≈ 6.2 k calls; decode inside the sitting's own drift (A's G=64 cell fell 2.6 % from start to end as the phone heated 29 → 58 °C); prefill DSP time +2.7 %, inside the gate. B (C's new binary on the unrotated model, so the flag is off) was byte-identical to A in text and PPL. Two rules came out of the gtests: v79's HVX IEEE float arithmetic keeps subnormal numbers instead of flushing them to zero, so the scalar reference must not flush either (the second rule 24 in the LEDGER), and a synthetic single-shape gtest can gate bit-exactness but not accuracy — its SNR said −5.8 dB while the full model said +8 dB (rule 25). Also measured for the first time: the NPU model's own PPL is 4.9 % above the CPU model's, and its text leaves the CPU's at generated word 43 — the size of the "n/a (different weights)" every NPU row carries. Verdict: carry it over; the port to htp_moe, including the GEMV path's requant site, is #110 (p2). 95-down-hadamard.md @ 3a566761 TPS, Accuracy, Profile, Verdict; LEDGER §2 ⑱, rules 24–25, §3 ⑱, upstream cycle 8; issue #110

#88 — wall 3 closes, the largest single gain so far (2026-09-22 15:35–16:20 KST, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at 3f9fa38d)

The question was whether upstream's two transport fixes, taken into htp_moe as PR #103 (merged by the user as ad714de7 before the sitting was filled), bring the decode MoE call's round trip under 0.1 ms. They did: M==1 transport 401.3 → 87.9 µs/call (−78.1 %) at profile level 2 (82.3 at level 3), qos_mode=2 in all four profiles, so LEDGER ⑦ closed on its "done" branch and the prebind step of plan 88 was not built. Variant C, the same binary with the poll window forced back to 100 µs, split the cut into 245.8 µs of staging (every call no longer cleans and invalidates a 4 MiB cached buffer pair sized by the prompt; the new staging: line shows decode riding a 64 KiB pair with 464 argument bytes outside ION) and 67.6 µs of poll window (the ARM now polls for 5 ms instead of 100 µs, so a ≈ 1.4 ms call no longer ends on the interrupt path) — plan 88's attribution in order and size, which also retired LEDGER ②'s older "marshalling / prebind" reading. Decode rose +30.9 / +41.4 / +42.9 % (18.72 / 16.83 / 16.46 → 24.50 / 23.80 / 23.52 tok/s), text byte-identical A = B = C at every G, prefill −0.9 % (gate −5 %) with prefill's own transport down 29 % and its DSP time unmoved; B ran hotter than A and still won every cell. B became the project's NPU "now" (distance to 50 tok/s at G=512: 3.0× → 2.1×). Two rules came out of it. Rule 22: the handoff's staging directory and worktrees did not exist on the workstation that ran it, so every binary was rebuilt from the named commits; a sitting like that is accepted when each results row carries the device md5 of the binary that differs, the commit diff is exactly the PR, and one skel serves all variants. Rule 23: #88's A and #94 sitting 2's A are the same sources on the same phone two hours apart, yet their transport columns read 401.3 vs 648.6 µs while dsp= agreed to 0.2 % — transport drifts across sittings like tok/s, so only dsp= is a cross-sitting column. And only ≈ 6.9 of the 12.6 ms/token gain at G=64 sits in the transport column; ≈ 5 ms landed outside the call's own timer, cause not decided. With the MoE call now 94 % DSP time, the per-token budget (above) names walls 1–2 as the biggest lever and resized ⑨ (one call per token) and ⑰ (the FC question). 88-moe-call-marshalling.md Why, Results, Transport table, Verdict, Notes (Deviations 1–2); LEDGER ⑦, ②, rules 22–23, §2 budget row, §3 ⑨ ⑰; BENCHMARK.md Log 2026-09-22 cycles 6–7; contract §1

#94 sitting 2 — the "now", the first lever, and where wall 2 sits (2026-09-22 13:40–14:35 KST, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at dad0f476)

The twelve A cells on one binary set replaced the provisional "now" (the NPU row was replaced again by #88 B the same afternoon and by #105 A later that day; the CPU row still stands): NPU 17.72 / 17.03 / 17.30 tok/s, CPU 52.43 / 49.22 / 48.31, NPU prefill 389–527 — 2.9× short of 50 at G=512 while the CPU sits on the ceiling. Variant C, the same binary with NNTR_MOE_HTP_M1_GEMV=1, was the first lever ever measured: decode +6.2 % (G=64), +7.7 % (G=512), +1.7 % (G=1024, inside the ±5 % no-change band), text byte-identical to A at every cell, the device gtest bit-identical; per M==1 call dsp 1414 → 1044 µs and the DMA ring gone (46 → 2 descriptors, wait 269 → 4.3 µs), but mm 783 → 975 µs because the GEMV now reads the arena directly at ≈ 22.6 GB/s — so the GEMV's weight feed is the next lever inside wall 1 (LEDGER ㉒, waits for #100), the path is to become the default (#101), and the M==1 profile print under it needs fixing (#102). Measurement B gave the first DMA ring: reading: the engine runs at 21.9–32.3 GB/s while busy, depth 9–11, only the first wait blocks — refuting "serialised behind compute" (a), the shape (b), the chain (c), and an intra-call DVFS ramp (e, the first expert is the fastest); the un-hidden first expert (f) and the cold-call penalty (g) are real but only ≈ 20 % of dsp_us together. The finding the device added is row h: the real 46-descriptor / 32-region list, replayed with no compute, no pacing and no competitor, runs at 40.2 GB/s where a probe of the identical per-descriptor shape does 106.9 on the same unit and skel — 2.7× lost inside the descriptor list or region layout itself, ≈ 60–70 % of the isolated-vs-in-situ gap. That became the wall-2 fix issue, #100 (gate: replay ≥ 80 % of probe iii, in-situ engine ≥ 40 GB/s, text identical), blocked by #99 because the replay harness failed its content check (below); row d stands as the constraint that whatever replaces the list must not become a DDR competitor of its own. (#100 then measured, on 2026-09-23, that the 106.9 was never a ceiling and the list is faster than the validated one — row h dissolved; the paragraph above.) Two rules were learned: probe rates drift 3–19 % with the phone's temperature inside one sitting (rule 18), and NPU tok/s drifts −7…−16 % day to day on one unit while the DSP profile columns move ≤ 4 % (rule 20). The skel that ran was the user's own build of the same DSP sources (the #98 fix only); the supervisor accepted it as the sitting's provenance because A and C were one binary set switched by an environment variable (rule 21). 94-sitting2-anchor-trace.md Results, Deviations 2, 5, 6, §A–§C, attribution and replay tables; LEDGER rules 18–21, §2 verdicts ⑯ and a–h, §3 ⑥ ⑳ ㉑ ㉒; BENCHMARK.md Log 2026-09-22 cycle 5b; issues #99 #100 #101 #102

Why #99 does not undo the replay numbers (plan 99, 2026-09-22)

Every one of the 11 MoeChunkReplay cells ended checksum_ok=n with the same two numbers (res[6] 1 467 840 vs want_sum 9 461 760, ratio 6.446), and bytes_per_call read 22 560 768 where the handoff expected 22 020 096. Plan 99 read the code and found three separate facts: (1) the kernel writes each gate/up chunk into VTCM strided — destination stride = source stride = 57 344 B, so an expert's eight chunks tile a 3 670 016 B window exactly once — while the replay passed destination stride = row size, so the chunks landed packed and overlapping in a 569 344 B window; the skel summed the packed window (8 896 samples × 0xA5 = 1 467 840) and the host the kernel's (57 344 × 0xA5 = 9 461 760); (2) 22 560 768 is correct — it is 22 020 096 of weights plus 524 288 of activation blocks plus 16 384 of copies, and the in-situ weight DMA: line counts weights only; (3) regions=32 is the fresh=1 rotation pool, not the expert count. The DDR read stream — source addresses, strides, row sizes, byte counts, order and the 30 wait points — was byte-for-byte the in-situ one; only the VTCM write geometry differed, and DMA_PROBE shape iii writes packed too, so packing cannot be what separates 40.2 from 106.9 GB/s. Under this diagnosis rows c, d, g and h stand as measured; the fix (replay with the kernel's destination stride, print weight_bytes beside bytes_per_call, a host check that pins the footprint arithmetic) rides along in a later handoff. Plan written, not yet implemented at ca9a46f7. #100's new cells (PR #112) side-stepped it by setting each descriptor's destination mode explicitly, and all 16 passed their content check in the same run in which the 11 old lines still failed at :633; the timings of the old cells therefore stand, but h's reading did not survive #100 (the comparison point, not the measurement, was wrong). docs/plans/99-replay-content-check.md §1, §3 "Root cause", "Verdict on the provisional rows"; issue #99; LEDGER ⑳

#94 first attempt — the skel that would not load (2026-09-22 morning, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at ec7ad296)

Six CPU control cells ran (53.0 / 53.5 · 51.1 / 51.2 · 49.7 / 48.4 tok/s, a same-unit re-run of #77's within ±4 %); every NPU cell was blocked because the skel built from 2a75f7d9 failed remote_handle_open_domain with 0x80000406 (dlerror RX VA 0xFFF00000 outside ELF segment). The cause was not a segment layout: PR #93's hmx/hexkl_dma_trace.c had never been added to SRCS in test/htp/build.sh, the Hexagon linker accepts a shared object with unresolved symbols, so -Wall -Werror stayed clean and the skel carried seven undefined hexkl_dma_trace_* functions that only the on-device loader rejects. #97 (p0) → PR #98 added the file and a post-link guard that prints UNDEFINED SYMBOLS OK (<n> runtime imports) or lists the offenders and exits 1 (LEDGER rule 17); sitting 2's first device command, DmaProbeShapes PASSED, was its acceptance on silicon. 94-sitting2-anchor-trace.md "First attempt" and Deviation 1; BENCHMARK.md Results (#94 partial rows) and Log cycle 5a; PR #98 body; LEDGER rule 17, ⑲

#77 — first handoff (2026-09-21, unit used R3CY205ZMND, filled at e8b930ad)

The control runs put the PR head under the project's fixed protocol (prompt 512, 8 threads, greedy): the NPU model decodes at 17.0–21.3 tok/s and the CPU model at 46.4–54.1, so the NPU was 2.7× short of the 50 tok/s goal at gen 512 while the CPU cleared it at gen 64 and 512 and missed it at 1024; NPU prefill is the one place the HTP wins (403–541 vs 231–336). The numbers were recorded unit-tagged and, at the time, left the PR author's "now" provisional pending a second-unit anchor sitting (#91); that requirement was withdrawn on 2026-09-22 (any unit, same-sitting A/B only) and sitting 2 replaced the "now" instead. Measurement A (the profile build) showed that MoE plus the dense FCs are 92 % of the NPU token and that the MoE on the NPU costs ~2× the CPU path per token, which ranks the levers: the MoE call first, round trips second, lm_head not first. Measurement B settled wall 3: the transport stays at 527.7 µs when the DSP is kept hot (level 3), so it is not wake-up; plan 88 later attributed it to cache maintenance on the over-sized staging pair plus the poll-window fallback. Measurement C rewrote wall 2: the DMA engine reaches 72–117 GB/s in isolation on the same arena and skel, workers and the bus vote change nothing, so the 16–18 GB/s seen inside the layer call is a property of how the call uses the ring (#87 → PR #93 → sitting 2's row h). The two-reader probe's DSP side was invalid (rule 12, #90); its CPU side showed −41 % under contention. Three protocol defects were filed: the generation(last 64) line never prints for this model (#89), compiled artifacts are not byte-reproducible across build paths (rule 14), and NNTR_HTP_PROFILE=3 inflates [M0-PROF] ffn= ~5× (rule 15). 77-first-handoff.md Results and Deviations; LEDGER §1 rules 11–16, §2 verdicts ①–④, ⑮; BENCHMARK.md Log 2026-09-21; contract §12

PR-author rows (2026-09-16/17 and 2026-09-21, not handoffs)

These come from the upstream PR's own documents and set the starting point: with the MoE FFN on the HTP the PR measured decode 20.8 tok/s against a CPU 48 (doc 49 §1), and its decode anatomy (doc 48 §3) named the three walls. A later run with conv in_proj also on the HTP fell to 16.5 tok/s with no code cause and no control run in the sitting (doc 50 §3.4), which is why every sitting in this project starts with the control binary (LEDGER rule 9). These rows are never compared with the nntrainer rows. BENCHMARK.md Results rows 1–3; contract §1

Compiled artifacts are not byte-reproducible across build paths (rule 14), so a handoff's table md5 and the device md5sum differ by construction; the device lines against the commit are the provenance. Pushing an old app with a new skel, or the reverse, fails with AEE_EBADPARM (rule 3). Since #88 a handoff must be rebuildable from its commits on any workstation, and a rebuilt set is accepted under the three conditions of rule 22.
